### SESSION 5 — GRADED INEQUALITY AND THE FAILURE OF ANTI-CASTE SOLIDARITY

#### The picture first

```text
  NOT A BINARY. AN ASCENDING SCALE OF REVERENCE AND A DESCENDING SCALE
  OF CONTEMPT, IN WHICH ALMOST EVERY STRATUM HAS SOMETHING TO LOSE.
  ====================================================================

              highest reverence
                     ^
                     |     EACH LEVEL:
                     |       subordinated to those ABOVE
                     |       SUPERIOR to those BELOW
                     |       -> therefore holds a STAKE in the
                     |          continuance of the whole order
                     v
              deepest contempt

  THE CONTRAST THAT MAKES THE POINT VISIBLE
  +---------------------------------+---------------------------------+
  | A BINARY HIERARCHY              | A GRADED HIERARCHY              |
  +---------------------------------+---------------------------------+
  | two homogeneous groups          | many ranked groups              |
  | the subordinated share ONE      | each group's interest DIFFERS   |
  | interest -- to end the order    | from the group below it         |
  | solidarity is the natural       | solidarity requires each group  |
  | outcome of shared position      | to SURRENDER a relative gain    |
  | the order falls when the        | the order survives because      |
  | subordinated combine            | combination is against interest |
  +---------------------------------+---------------------------------+
        |
        v
  THE POLITICAL CONSEQUENCE THAT EARNS THE MARK
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | THE OPPRESSED DO NOT UNITE BECAUSE OPPRESSION IS DISTRIBUTED        |
  | THROUGH A LADDER RATHER THAN CONCENTRATED IN A BINARY. GRADED       |
  | INEQUALITY IS THEREFORE AN EXPLANATION OF CASTE'S DURABILITY, NOT   |
  | MERELY A DESCRIPTION OF ITS SHAPE.                                  |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |
   +----+----------------------------+------------------------------+
   | WHAT IT EXPLAINS                | WHAT IT DOES NOT SUPPLY      |
   | why isolated reforms are        | A REMEDY. It names a         |
   | absorbed; why class solidarity  | structural obstacle and      |
   | is obstructed; why sincere      | leaves open whether rights,  |
   | appeals to common humanity fail | representation or conversion |
   | without changing anything       | is the correct response      |
   +---------------------------------+------------------------------+
```

*Caption: graded inequality drawn as a ladder rather than a binary, the four-row contrast that shows why solidarity is structurally obstructed rather than merely absent, the political consequence that converts the concept from description into explanation, and the explicit statement of what the concept does not supply.*

#### In plain words

The most natural picture of an unjust hierarchy has two levels: the powerful and the subordinated. On that picture, the road to change is obvious — the subordinated share a position, therefore share an interest, therefore combine.

Caste does not have that shape. It has many ranked levels, and almost every level is subordinated to those above while claiming superiority over those below. That single structural fact changes everything about the politics. A group in the middle of the order is genuinely oppressed and also genuinely holds something — a relative standing, a claim to precedence, a distinction from those beneath — that combination with the groups below would require it to surrender. Solidarity is therefore not merely absent; it is **against the interest** that the order itself has manufactured.

⚠️ This is why appeals to common humanity can be entirely sincere and entirely ineffective. The appeal asks each level to give up something it is holding; the order has arranged matters so that each level has something to hold. And it is why isolated reforms are absorbed: removing one disability at one level rearranges the ladder without dismantling the principle that produces ladders.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **Graded inequality.** Caste is not a simple binary between two homogeneous classes. It is a graded order in which groups may be subordinated to some while claiming superiority over others — an **ascending scale of reverence and a descending scale of contempt** in which each stratum has an interest in the order's continuance.

✅ **The political consequence.** Graded hierarchy **obstructs solidarity** because each level has a stake in distinction from those below. This is the owner's own statement, and it is the load-bearing claim: the concept explains caste's durability rather than merely describing its shape.

⚠️ **What it explains.** Why isolated reforms are absorbed rather than accumulating; why class solidarity across caste lines has been so difficult to build; why moral appeal, however sincere, has repeatedly failed to produce combination among the subordinated.

⚠️ **What it does not supply.** A remedy. It names a structural obstacle and leaves entirely open whether enforceable rights, autonomous representation, inter-caste marriage or exit from a caste-sanctioning religion is the correct response. Session 14 supplies the answer Ambedkar gives; the concept itself does not entail it.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **Ambedkar's graded inequality** ✅ — the ascending scale of reverence and descending scale of contempt; the standard explanation of why anti-caste mobilisation has repeatedly failed to unify.
- **The stake-in-continuance claim** ✅ — the owner's statement that each level has an interest in distinction from those below, which converts an observation about shape into an explanation of durability.
- **The absorption of isolated reform** ⚠️ — the analytical consequence that links graded inequality directly to Ambedkar's argument that reform of a single component leaves the normative foundation intact.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Caste endures not because the oppressed are powerless but because oppression is distributed through a ladder rather than concentrated in a binary: each level is subordinated above and superior below, so combination with those beneath requires surrendering the one distinction the order has left it.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**ascending scale of reverence** · **descending scale of contempt** · **stake in the order's continuance** · **ladder rather than binary** · **solidarity structurally obstructed** · **isolated reform absorbed** · **durability explained, not described** · **no remedy entailed** · **relative standing surrendered** · **each level oppressed and interested**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Caste's durability is explained not by the strength of those at the top but by its graded form, which distributes oppression through a ladder and thereby manufactures an interest in the order's continuance at almost every level of it. **Named evidence.** Ambedkar describes caste as an ascending scale of reverence and a descending scale of contempt, in which a group may be subordinated to those above while claiming superiority over those below; the owner draws the consequence explicitly, that graded hierarchy obstructs solidarity because each level has a stake in distinction from those beneath it. **Analysis.** The contrast with a binary hierarchy makes the point testable. Where two homogeneous groups face each other, the subordinated share a position and therefore an interest, and combination is the natural outcome; where many ranked groups face each other, combination requires each group to surrender a relative standing the order has given it, so solidarity is not merely absent but against manufactured interest — which is why sincere appeals to common humanity have repeatedly failed and why the removal of one disability at one level rearranges the ladder without disturbing the principle that produces ladders. **Qualification.** The concept explains an obstacle and entails no remedy: it does not by itself establish whether enforceable rights, autonomous representation, the breaking of the marriage circle or exit from a caste-sanctioning religion is the correct response, and an answer that treats graded inequality as though it argued for any one of them has converted a diagnosis into a conclusion it cannot support.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection.** ✅ The graded model over-predicts stability. Anti-caste movements have in fact formed, alliances across ranked groups have in fact been built, and subordinated groups have in fact combined electorally. If the structure made solidarity contrary to interest, none of that should have occurred.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ The claim is about the **default tendency** of the structure, not about impossibility. What the model predicts is that solidarity will be **costly and unstable** — requiring each participating group to accept the loss of a relative standing — and therefore that it must be built and sustained by deliberate organisation rather than expected to arise from shared position. That is precisely why Ambedkar's programme runs through **organisation** and autonomous political capacity rather than through appeals to a spontaneous common interest.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ The model does not specify **which** groups will combine, under what conditions, or for how long, and it offers no way to weigh the relative pull of graded standing against shared subordination in any particular case. Those are empirical questions about which this owner asserts nothing, and a script that predicts political outcomes from the model has exceeded what the concept licenses.

#### Examiner traps on this session

17. Converting graded inequality into a two-class model borrowed from class analysis. The ladder, not the binary, is the load-bearing claim, and the borrowed model destroys the explanation of durability.
18. Using the concept as a description of shape. Its value is explanatory: it accounts for absorbed reform and obstructed solidarity, and a script that only reports the shape has taken the weakest available reading.
19. Treating the concept as an argument for a particular remedy. It names an obstacle; the remedy is contested, and Ambedkar's answer is a further argument rather than a corollary.
20. Predicting political behaviour from the model. It states a structural tendency and cannot license claims about which groups combine, when, or with what result.

#### Mini recap

One shape: many ranked levels, each subordinated above and superior below. One consequence: each level holds a stake in the order's continuance, so solidarity requires surrendering a relative standing. One explanatory yield: absorbed reform, obstructed combination, sincere and ineffective moral appeal. One boundary: the concept explains durability and entails no remedy. One caution: it states a default tendency, not an impossibility, which is why organisation rather than shared position is Ambedkar's route.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 5
  graded inequality -> an ascending scale of reverence and a descending
      scale of contempt, not a binary of two homogeneous groups
      -> MECHANISM: each level is subordinated above and superior below,
         so combination with those beneath costs a relative standing
      -> CONSEQUENCE: solidarity is structurally obstructed, isolated
         reform is absorbed, and moral appeal fails without changing
         anything
      -> TRAP: importing a two-class model; using the concept as
         description; reading a remedy out of a diagnosis
      -> ANSWER USE: deploy to explain DURABILITY, then hand over to the
         method contest, which the concept does not settle
```

### SESSION 6 — CASTE UNDER UNIVERSAL FRANCHISE: THE BODY POLITIC

#### The picture first

```text
  THE 2022 Q1(b) STEM, DRAWN AS A TENSION RATHER THAN AS A TOPIC
  ==============================================================
  "In the age of individualism and universal franchise, what role does
   caste play in body-politic?"
        |
   +----+---------------------------------+-------------------------+
   | WHAT THE FRANCHISE GENUINELY ENABLES | WHAT IT DOES NOT DISSOLVE|
   +--------------------------------------+-------------------------+
   | subordinated populations become      | candidate selection      |
   | political CONSTITUENCIES             | patronage                |
   | voice, bargaining and policy         | symbolic mobilisation    |
   | attention become available           | social blocs             |
   | organisation converts scattered      | UNEQUAL SOCIAL STATUS    |
   | suffering into representation        | the marriage circle      |
   +--------------------------------------+-------------------------+
        |
        v
  THE SEPARATION THAT MUST BE STATED -- NOT ALL CASTE POLITICS IS ONE THING
  +--------------+-------------------------------+---------------------+
  | ASSERTION    | seeks EQUALITY, voice and     | normatively         |
  |              | equal citizenship             | DEFENSIBLE          |
  +--------------+-------------------------------+---------------------+
  | SUPREMACY    | seeks INHERITED PRIVILEGE and | normatively         |
  |              | preserved rank                | INDEFENSIBLE        |
  +--------------+-------------------------------+---------------------+
  | ELECTORAL    | ordinarily contains elements  | JUDGE BY PROGRAMME  |
  | MOBILISATION | of BOTH                       | AND EFFECT, never   |
  |              |                               | by label            |
  +--------------+-------------------------------+---------------------+
        |
        v
  THE CONTRADICTION THAT CLOSES ALMOST EVERY TWENTY-MARKER
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | ONE PERSON ONE VOTE AND ONE VOTE ONE VALUE, SET AGAINST A SOCIAL    |
  | ORDER OF GRADED WORTH, IS A CONTRADICTION A POLITY CANNOT           |
  | INDEFINITELY BEAR. POLITICAL DEMOCRACY REQUIRES SOCIAL DEMOCRACY.   |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |
        v
  WHY INDIVIDUALISM DOES NOT SETTLE IT EITHER
  the franchise reaches THE CITIZEN, one at a time, at the ballot;
  ENDOGAMY, RITUAL RANK AND MATERIAL DEPENDENCE reach THE HOUSEHOLD,
  every day. An individualist premise does not dissolve a structure that
  operates through marriage, kinship and inherited standing.
```

*Caption: the 2022 body-politic stem drawn as a tension — what the franchise enables against what it leaves standing — with the assertion/supremacy/mobilisation separation, Ambedkar's contradiction between political and social democracy, and the reason an individualist premise does not by itself reach a structure that operates through the household.*

#### In plain words

Universal franchise is a genuine transformation and it is not a solvent. It converts populations that were objects of policy into **constituencies** — groups whose votes must be sought, whose grievances acquire political weight, and whose organisation can compel attention. That is not a small thing, and a script that treats the franchise as cosmetic has misdescribed the change.

But the franchise operates on one relation only: the relation between the individual citizen and the state, exercised periodically at the ballot. Caste operates on a different set of relations — who may marry whom, whose cooking may be eaten, whose labour is stigmatised, who owns land and who depends on the owner — and those operate continuously and inside the household. So the arrival of equal political weight does not entail the arrival of equal social standing, and the two can coexist for a very long time.

⚠️ Worse, the franchise supplies caste with new instruments. Candidate selection can be organised by birth-group; patronage can be distributed through it; symbols can mobilise it; and social blocs can be assembled from it. The mistake, however, is to conclude that all such politics is one thing. A group organising to end its own exclusion and a group organising to preserve inherited precedence are doing normatively opposite things with the same electoral tools, and an answer that condemns both as "casteism" has abandoned the distinction that the whole clause rests on.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **What the franchise enables.** Universal franchise converts subordinated populations into political constituencies and can enable representation, bargaining and policy attention.

⚠️ **What it does not dissolve.** Caste can persist through candidate selection, patronage, symbolic mobilisation and social blocs. Political democracy gives equal votes; caste society distributes unequal status and social power.

✅ **The normative separation, stated in three terms.** *Assertion* seeks equality and voice and is defensible. *Supremacy* seeks inherited privilege and is indefensible. *Electoral mobilisation* can contain elements of both and **must be judged by programme and effect**, not by the presence of caste in politics as such.

✅ **Ambedkar's contradiction.** A polity that grants one person one vote and one vote one value while sustaining graded social worth carries a contradiction it cannot indefinitely bear. ⚠️ This is a diagnosis of instability, not a prediction of collapse, and it is the reason the answer to the body-politic stem must run through **social democracy** rather than through electoral description.

⚠️ **Why individualism does not settle it.** The individualist premise addresses the citizen; the caste mechanism addresses the household. Marriage, kinship, inherited occupation and material dependence are not individual transactions, so a premise about individuals does not by itself reach them.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **The franchise-as-constituency argument** ✅ — the owner's positive account of what universal franchise makes available, which prevents the answer from collapsing into a claim that nothing has changed.
- **The assertion / supremacy / mobilisation separation** ✅ — the owner's own three-way normative distinction, and the single most important protection against condemning oppressed-caste claim-making.
- **Ambedkar's one-person-one-vote-one-vote-one-value contradiction** ✅ — the closing move of nearly every twenty-marker on this clause, developed in Session 15.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Universal franchise reaches the citizen at the ballot while caste reaches the household every day, which is why equal political weight and unequal social worth can coexist indefinitely — and why the role of caste in the body politic must be judged by whether a mobilisation seeks equal standing or preserves inherited precedence.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**subordinated populations become constituencies** · **candidate selection and patronage** · **symbolic mobilisation and social blocs** · **assertion against supremacy** · **judge by programme and effect** · **equal votes, unequal status** · **one person one vote, one vote one value** · **political democracy requires social democracy** · **the citizen against the household** · **instability rather than collapse**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Universal franchise changes the political standing of subordinated groups without dissolving the social structure that subordinates them, so caste's role in the body politic is neither residual nor unchanged: it is transformed into a resource that can be used for opposite normative purposes. **Named evidence.** The franchise converts subordinated populations into constituencies whose votes must be sought and whose organisation can compel policy attention; equally, caste persists through candidate selection, patronage, symbolic mobilisation and social blocs, and Ambedkar's formulation of the contradiction — one person one vote and one vote one value set against a social order of graded worth — states the resulting instability precisely. **Analysis.** The decisive move is the three-way separation that follows: assertion by oppressed castes seeks equality and voice and is normatively defensible; supremacy seeks inherited privilege and is not; and ordinary electoral mobilisation ordinarily contains elements of both and must therefore be judged by its programme and effect rather than by the mere presence of caste in politics. Without that separation, an answer either celebrates all caste mobilisation or condemns all of it, and each of those is a failure to adjudicate. **Qualification.** The contradiction Ambedkar names is a diagnosis of instability rather than a prediction of collapse, and the individualist premise in the stem does not settle the matter either — the franchise addresses the citizen at the ballot while endogamy, ritual rank and material dependence address the household continuously, so a premise about individuals does not by itself reach a structure that reproduces through marriage and kinship.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection.** ✅ If caste organises voting, candidate selection and patronage, then democratic politics is entrenching caste rather than eroding it, and the honest conclusion is that universal franchise has strengthened the very structure it was expected to dissolve.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ This confuses two things the owner separates. Caste-based **domination** and caste-based **claim-making** use the same electoral instruments and stand in opposite normative relations to equality. Where organisation converts scattered suffering into representation, the franchise is doing what Ambedkar wanted it to do; where it distributes patronage and preserves precedence, it is not. The test is the programme and its effect, and a blanket verdict on "caste in politics" cannot be delivered without applying it.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ The test is genuinely hard to apply in particular cases, because a single mobilisation may simultaneously enlarge a subordinated group's voice and consolidate an elite inside it. **Elite capture** and permanent bloc politics are real risks that this owner names and does not resolve, and an answer that presents assertion as automatically emancipatory has ignored a residue that the owner itself concedes.

#### Examiner traps on this session

21. Concluding that the franchise has made no difference. It converts populations into constituencies with bargaining power, and denying that makes Ambedkar's own insistence on representation unintelligible.
22. Condemning all caste mobilisation as casteism. It erases the assertion/supremacy separation and ends by criticising the political self-organisation of the oppressed.
23. Answering the stem with electoral description. The marks are in the contradiction between equal votes and graded worth, not in an account of how elections are fought.
24. Presenting the contradiction as a prediction that caste will disappear. It is a diagnosis of instability; the owner asserts no timeline and no outcome.

#### Mini recap

One transformation: subordinated populations become constituencies with voice, bargaining power and policy attention. One persistence: candidate selection, patronage, symbolic mobilisation, social blocs and unequal social status. One separation: assertion is defensible, supremacy is not, and ordinary mobilisation contains both and must be judged by programme and effect. One contradiction: equal votes against graded worth, which political democracy cannot indefinitely bear. One reason individualism does not settle it: the franchise reaches the citizen; endogamy and dependence reach the household.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 6
  universal franchise -> converts subordinated populations into political
      constituencies with voice, bargaining and policy attention
      -> MECHANISM: the franchise reaches the CITIZEN at the ballot;
         endogamy, ritual rank and dependence reach the HOUSEHOLD daily
      -> CONSEQUENCE: equal votes and unequal social worth coexist, and
         caste acquires new instruments -- candidate selection, patronage,
         symbols, blocs
      -> TRAP: denying the change; condemning all caste mobilisation;
         answering with electoral description
      -> ANSWER USE: separate assertion from supremacy, judge by
         programme and effect, close on political-versus-social democracy
```

### SESSION 7 — FOUR DIMENSIONS OF CASTE INJUSTICE, AND CASTE WITH CLASS, GENDER AND RELIGION

#### The picture first

```text
  FOUR DIMENSIONS, FOUR MECHANISMS, FOUR REMEDIES -- NOT SUBSTITUTES
  ==================================================================

  +----------------+---------------------------+----------------------+
  | DIMENSION      | THE CASTE MECHANISM       | THE REQUIRED         |
  |                |                           | RESPONSE             |
  +----------------+---------------------------+----------------------+
  | REDISTRIBUTION | unequal land, labour,     | resources and        |
  |                | education, networks and   | institutional access |
  |                | material security         |                      |
  +----------------+---------------------------+----------------------+
  | RECOGNITION /  | stigma, purity/pollution, | repudiation of       |
  | STATUS         | humiliation and graded    | inherited rank and   |
  |                | social worth              | equal standing       |
  +----------------+---------------------------+----------------------+
  | REPRESENTATION | others speak for the      | autonomous voice and |
  |                | group; candidate and      | political safeguards |
  |                | office access controlled  |                      |
  |                | elsewhere                 |                      |
  +----------------+---------------------------+----------------------+
  | RELIGION       | social hierarchy presented| freedom of conscience|
  |                | as SACRED AUTHORITY       | PLUS rejection of    |
  |                |                           | civic disability     |
  +----------------+---------------------------+----------------------+
        |
   MEDIATED THROUGHOUT BY TWO FURTHER AXES
  +----------------+---------------------------+----------------------+
  | GENDER         | ENDOGAMY regulates        | anti-caste and       |
  |                | marriage, sexuality and   | gender analysis      |
  |                | property                  | JOINTLY structured   |
  +----------------+---------------------------+----------------------+
  | CLASS          | exploitation INTERACTS    | material analysis    |
  |                | with, but does not        | WITHOUT reducing     |
  |                | EXHAUST, status closure   | caste to class       |
  +----------------+---------------------------+----------------------+
        |
        v
  THE TWO FAILURE MODES THIS GRID NAMES
   IDENTITY WITHOUT REDISTRIBUTION -> material power is left intact
   REDISTRIBUTION WITHOUT RECOGNITION -> humiliation is preserved
        |
        v
  THE ARITHMETIC ERROR THAT MUST BE REFUSED
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | DALIT WOMEN'S POSITION IS NOT THE SUM OF CASTE ANALYSED SEPARATELY  |
  | PLUS GENDER ANALYSED SEPARATELY. ENDOGAMY MAKES CASTE CONTINUITY    |
  | DEPEND ON THE REGULATION OF WOMEN'S MARRIAGE, SO THE TWO ARE        |
  | JOINTLY STRUCTURED AT THE MECHANISM AND NOT ADDED AFTERWARDS.       |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

*Caption: the four dimensions of caste injustice with the mechanism each names and the remedy each requires, the two mediating axes of gender and class, the two failure modes the grid exposes, and the refusal of the arithmetic reading of caste and gender.*

#### In plain words

Injustice of this kind is not one thing needing one remedy. Ask what is wrong when a person is denied land, denied respect, denied a voice, and told that the denial is sacred, and you have named four different wrongs. They travel together and they do not reduce to one another.

**Redistribution** answers the material dimension: unequal holdings of land, labour, education, networks and security. **Recognition** answers status: the stigma, the ritual rules of pollution, the humiliation and the assignment of graded social worth. **Representation** answers voice: the condition in which others speak for a group and control access to candidacy and office. **Religion** answers the sanction: hierarchy presented as sacred, which requires the double response of protecting freedom of conscience while denying that any doctrine may impose civic disability.

⚠️ Two further axes mediate all four. **Gender** is internal to the mechanism, because endogamy regulates marriage and sexuality and therefore makes caste continuity depend on the control of women's marriage choices. **Class** interacts with caste without exhausting it: control of land, labour and education is bound up with caste, and caste is nevertheless not a form of class, because it ranks persons by birth through a sacred sanction that no account of ownership supplies.

The two failure modes are what earn marks. Identity assertion **without** redistribution can leave material power exactly where it was; redistribution **without** recognition can transfer resources while preserving humiliation. Naming both is the mark of an answer that has understood why the dimensions are distinct.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **Redistribution.** The mechanism is unequal land, labour, education, networks and material security; the required response is resources and institutional access.

✅ **Recognition and status.** The mechanism is stigma, purity and pollution, humiliation and graded social worth; the required response is the repudiation of inherited rank and the establishment of equal standing.

✅ **Representation.** The mechanism is that others speak for subordinated groups or control candidate and office access; the required response is autonomous voice and political safeguards.

✅ **Religion.** The mechanism is that social hierarchy may be presented as sacred authority; the required response is freedom of conscience **plus** the rejection of civic disability.

✅ **Gender.** Endogamy regulates marriage, sexuality and property; anti-caste and gender analysis must therefore be **jointly structured**. ⚠️ Dalit women's experience challenges any analysis that adds caste and gender as separate burdens after first treating each in isolation. The full gender doctrine is routed to its own owner and never developed here.

✅ **Class.** Caste interacts with control of land, labour and education, but is **not reducible to class**; the required response combines material analysis without reduction.

⚠️ **The two failure modes.** Identity without redistribution leaves material power intact; redistribution without recognition preserves humiliation. The remedies are analytically distinct and practically entangled.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **The four-dimension grid** ✅ — the canonical owner's own table of dimension, mechanism and required response; the device that prevents an answer from proposing one remedy for four different wrongs.
- **The endogamy–gender bridge** ✅ — the claim that caste continuity depends heavily on regulating women's marriage and sexuality, which is Ambedkar's own 1916 consequence and the reason the joint structuring is doctrinal rather than merely political.
- **The two failure modes** ⚠️ — the owner's statement that identity without redistribution leaves material power intact while redistribution without recognition preserves humiliation.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Caste inflicts four distinguishable wrongs — material deprivation, denied standing, silenced voice and sanctified rank — and because each has its own mechanism, a remedy that answers one of them can leave the other three exactly as they were.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**redistribution, recognition, representation, religion** · **stigma and graded social worth** · **others speak for the group** · **sacred authority and civic disability** · **endogamy regulates marriage and sexuality** · **jointly structured, not arithmetically added** · **interacts with class without reduction** · **identity without redistribution** · **redistribution without recognition** · **distinct in analysis, entangled in practice**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Caste inflicts four analytically distinct wrongs, and because each has its own mechanism, no single remedy answers all four — which is why proposals that appear to compete are frequently addressing different dimensions of the same structure. **Named evidence.** The owner separates them explicitly: unequal land, labour, education and security call for resources and institutional access; stigma, purity and pollution and graded worth call for the repudiation of inherited rank; the condition in which others speak for a group and control office access calls for autonomous voice and political safeguards; and hierarchy presented as sacred authority calls for freedom of conscience together with the refusal of civic disability. **Analysis.** Separating them produces two immediately usable results. First, the failure modes become nameable: identity assertion without redistribution can leave material power untouched, and redistribution without recognition can transfer resources while preserving humiliation, so an answer can specify exactly what a proposed remedy will and will not achieve. Second, the Gandhi–Ambedkar disagreement becomes locatable — Gandhi's method concentrates on recognition and, through trusteeship, on part of redistribution, while Ambedkar's insists that representation and the sanction must be attacked directly. **Qualification.** The dimensions are analytically distinct and practically entangled, and two mediating axes cut across all four: endogamy makes caste continuity depend on the regulation of women's marriage and sexuality, so caste and gender are jointly structured at the mechanism rather than added afterwards; and caste interacts with the control of land, labour and education without being reducible to class, since no account of ownership supplies the sacred sanction that ranks persons by birth.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection.** ✅ The grid multiplies categories without adding anything. Every one of the four "dimensions" is ultimately about power, and dividing power into material, symbolic, political and religious components produces a taxonomy that describes the same domination four times.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ The division earns its keep by predicting **failure**. It is precisely because the dimensions are distinct that a remedy succeeding in one can leave the others intact — resources transferred while humiliation continues, presence secured while agendas are set elsewhere, ritual practice prohibited while the sanction survives. A single undifferentiated concept of power cannot state those outcomes, and stating them is what an evaluative stem asks for.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ The grid does not establish **priority** among the four. Whether recognition must precede redistribution, or representation must precede both, is contested and is not settled by the distinction itself; the owner records the entanglement and stops. A script that reads a ranking out of the grid has asserted more than the source supports.

#### Examiner traps on this session

25. Adding caste and gender arithmetically. Endogamy makes them jointly structured at the mechanism, so "Dalit women face two burdens" is analytically wrong as well as thin.
26. Reducing caste to class. Interaction is not identity, and a script that treats caste as a form of class cannot explain the sacred sanction or the ranking of persons by birth.
27. Treating the four dimensions as substitutes. The failure modes exist precisely because they are not, and naming them is where the evaluative marks are.
28. Developing the gender doctrine or the recognition doctrine here. Both are named and routed; expounding either breaches the fence and displaces Gandhi and Ambedkar, who are what the clause prints.

#### Mini recap

Four dimensions: redistribution answers material deprivation, recognition answers denied standing, representation answers silenced voice, religion answers sanctified rank. Two mediating axes: gender is internal to endogamy, class interacts without exhausting. Two failure modes: identity without redistribution, redistribution without recognition. One refusal: caste and gender are jointly structured, not arithmetically added. One boundary: the grid names distinctions and does not rank them.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 7
  four dimensions of caste injustice -> redistribution, recognition,
      representation and religion, each with its own mechanism
      -> MECHANISM: gender is INTERNAL to endogamy and class INTERACTS
         without exhausting, so both mediate all four dimensions
      -> CONSEQUENCE: a remedy answering one dimension can leave the
         other three intact -- identity without redistribution,
         redistribution without recognition
      -> TRAP: adding caste and gender arithmetically; reducing caste to
         class; treating the dimensions as substitutes
      -> ANSWER USE: locate each thinker's method on the grid, then state
         precisely which dimension his remedy leaves unreached
```

### SESSION 8 — GANDHI I: PERIODISING THE POSITION, AND THE RESIDUE THAT SURVIVES

#### The picture first

```text
  NEVER FLATTEN GANDHI INTO ONE TIMELESS POSITION
  ===============================================
  THE PERIODISATION IS ITSELF A MARK-BEARING MOVE, AND IT IS THE FIRST
  THING THE 2022 AND 2025 STEMS REWARD.

  PHASE 1 . EARLIER
      distinguishes an IDEALISED HEREDITARY DUTY-ORDER from caste
      hierarchy; presents it as NON-COMPETITIVE, WITHOUT SUPERIORITY --
      a scheme of DUTIES rather than of RANKS
                    |
                    v
  PHASE 2 . THE ANTI-UNTOUCHABILITY CAMPAIGN
      condemns exclusion as a sin; TEMPLE ENTRY; shared SANITATION WORK
      and common service; the term "HARIJAN" is introduced --
      LATER REJECTED BY MANY DALIT THINKERS AS PATERNALISTIC
                    |
                    v
  PHASE 3 . THE LATER MOVEMENT
      increasingly critical of BIRTH-BASED CASTE BARRIERS; increasingly
      supportive of INTER-CASTE MARRIAGE
                    |
                    v
  THE RESIDUE -- WHAT PHASE 3 DOES NOT CANCEL
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | AN IDEALISED DUTY-ORDER CAN STILL ALLOCATE BY BIRTH EVEN AFTER      |
  | EXPLICIT RANKING IS DENIED. THAT IS A GENUINE THEORETICAL           |
  | LIABILITY AND CHRONOLOGY DOES NOT ARGUE IT AWAY.                    |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+

  THE PERIODISATION CONTROL -- THE QUALIFICATION THAT MUST BE WRITTEN
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | THIS IS A TRAJECTORY RECONSTRUCTED ACROSS DECADES OF WRITING AND    |
  | PRACTICE. IT IS NOT A CLAIM THAT ONE DATE OR ONE SENTENCE CLEANLY   |
  | REPLACED EVERY EARLIER COMMITMENT. STATE THE LATER OPPOSITION TO    |
  | BIRTH BARRIERS TOGETHER WITH -- NOT INSTEAD OF -- THE EARLIER       |
  | LIABILITY.                                                          |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+

  TERM CONTROL
  "HARIJAN" IS GANDHI'S HISTORICAL USAGE, CONTESTED AT THE TIME AND
  SINCE. IT IS NEVER THE NARRATOR'S NEUTRAL TERM, AND IT IS NEVER
  PRESENTED AS AN UNCONTESTED EMANCIPATORY COINAGE.
```

*Caption: Gandhi's position drawn as three phases with the residue that the third phase does not cancel, the periodisation control that prevents the trajectory from being read as a clean replacement, and the terminological control on "Harijan".*

#### In plain words

The commonest failure on Gandhi is not hostility or admiration; it is **flattening**. A script decides in advance whether Gandhi was a reformer or an obstacle and then reads fifty years of writing as though it said one thing.

The accurate account has three phases. In the **earlier** phase Gandhi distinguishes an idealised hereditary duty-order from caste hierarchy: on his presentation it is non-competitive, involves no assertion of superiority, and allocates duties rather than ranks. In the phase of the **anti-untouchability campaign** he condemns exclusion in the strongest available terms, promotes temple entry, takes up sanitation work and common service himself, and introduces the term "Harijan" — a term later rejected by many Dalit thinkers as paternalistic. In the **later** phase his position becomes increasingly critical of birth-based caste barriers and increasingly supportive of inter-caste marriage.

⚠️ Two controls must accompany the periodisation. The first is that the trajectory is reconstructed across decades of writing and practice; it is not a claim that a date or a sentence cleanly replaced every earlier commitment, and an answer that presents the third phase as a repudiation of the first has overstated the evidence. The second is the residue: an idealised duty-order can still allocate by birth after explicit ranking has been denied, and that liability is not cancelled by the later opposition to birth barriers. Both must be written. Stating only the later position is apologetics; stating only the earlier is caricature.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **Phase 1 — the earlier position.** Gandhi distinguished an idealised hereditary duty-order (*varṇa*) from caste hierarchy, presenting it as non-competitive and without superiority.

✅ **Phase 2 — the anti-untouchability campaign.** He condemned exclusion, promoted temple entry and sanitation work, and used the term "Harijan," ⚠️ later rejected by many Dalit thinkers as paternalistic.

✅ **Phase 3 — the later movement.** His position became increasingly critical of birth-based caste barriers and more supportive of inter-caste marriage.

⚠️ **The residue.** The controversy remains because an idealised duty-order **can preserve birth allocation even after explicit ranking is denied**. This is the theoretical liability that the whole Ambedkarite objection turns on, and it is not removed by the later trajectory.

⚠️ **The periodisation control.** This is a trajectory reconstructed across writings and practice, not a claim that one date or sentence cleanly replaced every earlier commitment. Later opposition to birth barriers must be stated **together with**, not used to erase, the liability of the earlier hereditary-duty arguments.

⚠️ **The terminological control.** "Harijan" is Gandhi's historical usage and is contested; it is never the narrator's neutral term and never an uncontested emancipatory coinage.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **The three-phase periodisation** ✅ — the canonical owner's own reconstruction, and the single most reliable way to separate a script from the field on the 2022 and 2025 stems.
- **The residue of an idealised duty-order** ⚠️ — the owner's statement that inherited allocation can survive the denial of rank, which is the hinge of the entire Ambedkarite critique.
- **The contested status of "Harijan"** ⚠️ — the owner's caution, which is also the fastest available illustration of the paternalism objection developed in Session 11.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Gandhi's position on caste moved across decades and must be periodised rather than summarised: he condemned untouchability throughout, defended an idealised duty-order early, and moved late towards opposing birth barriers — yet the liability that an inherited allocation survives the denial of rank is not cancelled by that movement.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**periodise, never summarise** · **idealised hereditary duty-order** · **non-competitive and without superiority** · **temple entry and common service** · **"Harijan" as contested historical usage** · **later support for inter-caste marriage** · **birth allocation survives denied ranking** · **trajectory, not clean replacement** · **theoretical liability retained** · **apologetics against caricature**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Gandhi held no single timeless position on caste, and any evaluation that does not periodise his view will either defend a position he abandoned or attack one he reached, in both cases missing the target the stem sets. **Named evidence.** The trajectory has three identifiable phases: an earlier distinction between an idealised hereditary duty-order, presented as non-competitive and without assertion of superiority, and caste hierarchy proper; a sustained anti-untouchability campaign involving temple entry, shared sanitation work and common service, in which the contested term "Harijan" was introduced; and a later movement that grew increasingly critical of birth-based caste barriers and increasingly supportive of inter-caste marriage. **Analysis.** Periodising does two things at once. It states his position accurately, which is a precondition of evaluating it; and it locates the exact point at which the Ambedkarite objection bites — not on untouchability, which Gandhi condemned throughout, but on whether a duty-order that allocates by birth can be purified by denying that the allocation ranks anyone. That is the question the later phase moves towards and does not close. **Qualification.** Two controls are compulsory. The trajectory is reconstructed across decades of writing and practice rather than fixed by a date, so the later opposition to birth barriers must be stated together with, and never used to erase, the liability of the earlier arguments; and "Harijan" must be identified as Gandhi's historical usage, contested at the time and since, rather than adopted as the narrator's own neutral term.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection.** ✅ Periodisation is a way of protecting Gandhi. By dividing his position into phases, a script can always attribute the objectionable claims to an early Gandhi who was later superseded, which makes the position unfalsifiable and the evaluation empty.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ Only if the residue is dropped, which is precisely why the owner requires it to be stated. The periodisation is not offered as a defence but as an **accuracy condition**: it establishes what was held when, and it then insists that the later position does not cancel the earlier liability. An answer that periodises and then declines to name the residue has indeed produced apologetics; an answer that periodises and names it has produced an evaluation.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ The exact **dating and textual context** of particular formulations matter and are disputed among scholars. This owner reconstructs a trajectory and asserts no date for any specific change of view, and a Philosophy answer should say "earlier", "in the campaign" and "later" rather than invent chronological precision the source does not supply.

#### Examiner traps on this session

29. Flattening Gandhi into one position. It makes the 2022 stem unanswerable, because a critical evaluation requires knowing which position is being evaluated.
30. Inferring from "Gandhi opposed untouchability" that he opposed every form of the hereditary duty-order from the outset. That inference is exactly what the owner's third trap forbids.
31. Using "Harijan" as the narrator's neutral term. It must be identified as Gandhi's historical usage together with the contemporary caution.
32. Asserting dates for particular changes of view. The owner reconstructs a trajectory without dating individual formulations, and invented precision is an unsupported factual claim.

#### Mini recap

Three phases: the earlier idealised duty-order presented as duties without rank; the anti-untouchability campaign with temple entry, common service and the contested term "Harijan"; and the later movement towards opposing birth barriers and supporting inter-caste marriage. One residue: inherited allocation can survive the denial of rank. Two controls: the trajectory is reconstructed and not dated, and "Harijan" is historical usage rather than neutral vocabulary. One consequence: periodisation without the residue is apologetics; the residue without the periodisation is caricature.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 8
  Gandhi on caste -> THREE PHASES: idealised duty-order; the
      anti-untouchability campaign; the later anti-birth-barrier movement
      -> MECHANISM: the earlier position denies RANK while retaining
         allocation BY BIRTH, which is where the objection bites
      -> CONSEQUENCE: the later phase moves towards inter-caste marriage
         without cancelling the earlier theoretical liability
      -> TRAP: flattening the position; inferring blanket anti-varna from
         anti-untouchability; adopting "Harijan" as neutral usage
      -> ANSWER USE: periodise in line two, then name the residue -- both
         moves, or the evaluation is either apologetics or caricature
```

### SESSION 9 — GANDHI II: THE ARGUMENT AGAINST UNTOUCHABILITY IN FIVE STEPS

#### The picture first

```text
  THE DOCTRINE STATED AS AN ARGUMENT, NOT AS A SENTIMENT
  ======================================================

  STEP 1 . ALL LIFE PARTICIPATES IN A MORAL-SPIRITUAL UNITY
      therefore persons are of equal spiritual worth whatever their
      birth-group. THE PREMISE IS METAPHYSICAL, NOT SOCIOLOGICAL.
                    |
                    v
  STEP 2 . DEGRADING ANOTHER PERSON VIOLATES NON-VIOLENCE (AHIMSA)
           AND TRUTH
      untouchability is therefore not merely unkind; it BREACHES THE
      TWO COMMITMENTS on which the whole Gandhian ethic rests.
                    |
                    v
  STEP 3 . INHERITED EXCLUSION CORRUPTS BOTH PARTIES
      the excluded AND the conscience of the community that practises
      the exclusion. THE HARM IS TWO-WAY.
                    |
                    v
  STEP 4 . THEREFORE REFORM MUST INVOLVE REPENTANCE AND CHANGED
           CONDUCT BY THE PRIVILEGED
      RESPONSIBILITY IS PLACED ON THE DOMINANT, NOT ON THE VICTIM.
                    |
                    v
  STEP 5 . LEGAL PROHIBITION IS NECESSARY BUT CANNOT BY ITSELF
           GENERATE FELLOWSHIP
      law can forbid an act; it cannot manufacture regard.
        |
        v
  THE STRONGEST FORM OF THE CLAIM -- STATE IT AS A POSITION
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | UNTOUCHABILITY IS A SIN AGAINST HUMANITY AND AGAINST RELIGION       |
  | ITSELF, TO BE EXPIATED BY THE PRACTISING COMMUNITY THROUGH PENANCE, |
  | SERVICE AND SELF-PURIFICATION. NO RELIGIOUS SANCTION CAN ATTACH TO  |
  | THE DEGRADATION OF A HUMAN BEING.                                   |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |
        v
  THE LIMIT, STATED IMMEDIATELY -- DO NOT POSTPONE IT
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | THE ARGUMENT PLACES THE INITIATIVE WITH THE DOMINANT GROUP'S        |
  | CONSCIENCE. THAT IS PRECISELY AMBEDKAR'S CENTRAL OBJECTION, AND AN  |
  | ANSWER THAT STATES THE ARGUMENT WITHOUT THE LIMIT HAS WRITTEN HALF  |
  | OF IT.                                                              |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

*Caption: Gandhi's case against untouchability set out as a five-step argument from a metaphysical premise to a claim about the limits of law, the strongest formulation of the position, and the limit that must be stated in the same breath.*

#### In plain words

Gandhi's opposition to untouchability is often reported as a moral reaction. It is in fact an argument, and reporting it as a reaction throws away the marks.

It begins with a metaphysical premise: all life participates in a moral-spiritual unity, so no person can be of lower worth by birth. From that premise, degrading a person is not merely cruel but a violation of the two commitments that organise Gandhi's entire ethic — non-violence and truth. The third step is the one most often missed and the most distinctive: the harm runs **both ways**. Inherited exclusion corrupts the excluded and equally corrupts the conscience of the community practising the exclusion, which is why the wrong is not fully described by listing what the excluded suffer.

The fourth step follows directly. If the practising community is itself corrupted, then reform must involve its **repentance and changed conduct** — responsibility is placed on the dominant, not on the victim, and the demand is for penance, service and self-purification rather than for patience by the excluded. The fifth step marks the boundary of law: prohibition is necessary and it cannot by itself generate fellowship, because a rule can forbid an act and cannot manufacture regard.

⚠️ State the limit in the same breath. The argument places the initiative with the dominant group's conscience — and that is exactly what Ambedkar attacks. An answer that presents the five steps and then moves on has given the position without its vulnerability, which is not an evaluation.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **The doctrine statement.** Gandhi regards untouchability as a profound moral and religious wrong incompatible with truth, non-violence and the equal spiritual worth of persons. His remedy centres on self-purification, transformed social conscience, constructive work and non-violent reform.

✅ **The five steps.** (1) All life participates in a moral-spiritual unity. (2) Degrading another person violates non-violence (*ahiṃsā*) and truth. (3) Inherited exclusion corrupts both the oppressed and the caste Hindu conscience. (4) Social reform must therefore involve repentance and changed conduct by privileged castes. (5) Legal prohibition is necessary but cannot by itself generate fellowship.

✅ **The strongest formulation.** Untouchability is a sin against humanity and against religion itself, to be expiated by the practising community through penance, service and self-purification, and defended on the ground that no religious sanction can attach to the degradation of a human being.

⚠️ **The limit.** The argument places the initiative with the dominant group's conscience, which is Ambedkar's central objection. It must be stated with the argument and not postponed to a criticism section.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **The five-step argument** ✅ — the owner's own reconstruction, which converts a moral stance into a position that can be assessed premise by premise.
- **The two-way corruption claim** ✅ — the step that distinguishes Gandhi's account from a straightforward harm-to-victims argument and grounds the demand for penance by the privileged.
- **The necessity-but-insufficiency of law** ✅ — the fifth step, which is also the point of contact and of conflict with Ambedkar's constitutional programme.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> For Gandhi untouchability is not simply an injury inflicted on the excluded but a corruption of the community that inflicts it, which is why his remedy demands repentance and changed conduct from the privileged rather than patience from the victim — and why he holds that a law can forbid the act while leaving the regard it denies unmade.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**moral-spiritual unity** · **equal spiritual worth whatever the birth-group** · **non-violence (*ahiṃsā*) and truth** · **two-way corruption** · **repentance and changed conduct by the privileged** · **penance, service and self-purification** · **no sanction can attach to degradation** · **law necessary but not sufficient** · **initiative with the dominant conscience** · **fellowship cannot be legislated**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Gandhi's opposition to untouchability is an argument with a metaphysical premise and a conclusion about the limits of law, not a moral reaction, and stating it as an argument is what makes it assessable. **Named evidence.** The five steps run from the participation of all life in a moral-spiritual unity, through the claim that degrading a person violates non-violence and truth, to the distinctive third step that inherited exclusion corrupts both the excluded and the conscience of the community practising the exclusion; from that follows the demand that reform involve repentance and changed conduct by the privileged, and finally the boundary claim that legal prohibition is necessary but cannot by itself generate fellowship. **Analysis.** The third step is where the position earns its distinctiveness: because the harm is two-way, the appropriate response is not compensation to victims but expiation by the community, which is why penance, service and self-purification occupy the place that rights and representation occupy in Ambedkar. And the fifth step explains why Gandhi never treats legal abolition as the completion of the task — a rule can forbid an act while leaving unmade the regard whose absence produced the act. **Qualification.** The argument's structure is also its vulnerability, and the vulnerability must be stated with it rather than deferred: placing the initiative with the dominant group's conscience makes the remedy depend on the moral transformation of those who benefit from the arrangement, which is precisely Ambedkar's objection that justice resting on benevolence remains revocable.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection.** ✅ The metaphysical premise does too much work. If the argument depends on the participation of all life in a moral-spiritual unity, then anyone who rejects that metaphysics is untouched by the case against untouchability — which makes the strongest available moral condemnation hostage to a contested religious cosmology.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ The premise supplies Gandhi's own route, not the only route. The operative steps — that degradation violates non-violence and truth, that inherited exclusion corrupts the excluding community, and that law cannot manufacture regard — can be stated without the cosmology, and Gandhi's strategic purpose in using it was to argue **from within** the tradition that supplied the sanction, which is where the practice had to be dislodged.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ Arguing from within a tradition preserves the authority of that tradition, and Ambedkar's reply is that the authority structure itself must be broken rather than reinterpreted. Whether an internal reinterpretation can dislodge a sanction while leaving the interpretive authority intact is genuinely unresolved, and Session 11 treats it as a live objection rather than a settled one.

#### Examiner traps on this session

33. Reporting the position as moral outrage. Outrage is not assessable; the five steps are, and the marks are in the assessment.
34. Omitting the two-way corruption step. Without it the demand for penance by the privileged looks arbitrary rather than entailed.
35. Presenting Gandhi as indifferent to law. He holds prohibition to be **necessary** and insufficient, and the misreading destroys the contrast with Ambedkar by making it cruder than it is.
36. Postponing the limit to a criticism paragraph. The limit belongs with the argument, because the location of initiative is a structural feature of the position rather than an external complaint.

#### Mini recap

Five steps: moral-spiritual unity, degradation as a breach of non-violence and truth, two-way corruption, repentance and changed conduct by the privileged, and law as necessary but not sufficient. One strongest formulation: a sin against humanity and against religion itself, to be expiated by the practising community. One limit stated in the same breath: the initiative lies with the dominant conscience. One consequence: the demand is penance rather than patience, and fellowship rather than compliance.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 9
  Gandhi against untouchability -> a FIVE-STEP ARGUMENT, not a moral
      reaction
      -> MECHANISM: inherited exclusion corrupts BOTH the excluded and
         the conscience of the excluding community, so the remedy is
         expiation by that community
      -> CONSEQUENCE: repentance and changed conduct by the privileged;
         law necessary but unable to manufacture regard
      -> TRAP: reporting outrage; omitting two-way corruption; making
         Gandhi indifferent to law; postponing the limit
      -> ANSWER USE: state the five steps, then state in the same breath
         that the initiative rests with the dominant conscience
```

### SESSION 10 — GANDHI III: THE METHOD, ITS PRESUPPOSITIONS AND ITS ACHIEVEMENT

#### The picture first

```text
  METHOD IS WHERE GANDHI AND AMBEDKAR MOST SHARPLY DIVERGE -- AND IT IS
  THE COMPONENT MOST ROUTINELY OMITTED FROM SCRIPTS
  =====================================================================

  +----------------------------+--------------------------------------+
  | INSTRUMENT                 | WHAT IT IS SUPPOSED TO DO            |
  +----------------------------+--------------------------------------+
  | CHANGE OF HEART AND        | remove the INNER ASSENT on which the |
  | SELF-PURIFICATION          | practice depends                     |
  +----------------------------+--------------------------------------+
  | TRUTH-FORCE (SATYAGRAHA)   | contest unjust practice WITHOUT      |
  |                            | converting the opponent into an enemy|
  +----------------------------+--------------------------------------+
  | CONSTRUCTIVE PROGRAMME     | education, sanitation, common service|
  |                            | and village work as DAILY PRACTICE   |
  +----------------------------+--------------------------------------+
  | RELIGIOUS REINTERPRETATION | separate LIVING ETHICAL RELIGION from|
  |                            | unjust accretion, FROM WITHIN        |
  +----------------------------+--------------------------------------+
  | NON-VIOLENT PERSUASION     | refuse class war and coercive hatred |
  +----------------------------+--------------------------------------+
  | TRUSTEESHIP AND ECONOMIC   | reduce the DEPENDENCE through which  |
  | REFORM                     | caste power is actually exercised    |
  +----------------------------+--------------------------------------+
        |
        v
  THE FOUR PRESUPPOSITIONS THAT MAKE THE METHOD COHERENT
   P1 . religious traditions contain resources for SELF-CRITICISM
   P2 . privileged persons CAN be morally transformed
   P3 . MEANS MUST EMBODY the equal fellowship sought
   P4 . social unity must not be purchased through COERCIVE HATRED
        |
        v
  THE ACHIEVEMENT, STATED FAIRLY BEFORE ANY CRITIQUE
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | GANDHI BROUGHT CASTE HUMILIATION INTO MASS MORAL AND POLITICAL      |

  | WITHOUT INTERNAL MORAL REFORM WAS INCOMPLETE.                       |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |

  WHICH LINK OF THE ENGINE DOES THE METHOD REACH?
   M4 purity/pollution ....... REACHED -- directly, by temple entry and
                               shared service
   M6 material dependence .... PARTLY REACHED -- through trusteeship and
                               constructive work
   M1 the marriage circle .... REACHED ONLY LATE, through support for
                               inter-caste marriage
   M5 the sacred sanction .... NOT REACHED -- reinterpretation preserves
                               the interpretive authority
   REPRESENTATION ............ NOT ADDRESSED as an autonomous claim
```

*Caption: Gandhi's six instruments with the task each is meant to perform, the four presuppositions that make the method coherent, the achievement stated fairly before any criticism, and the diagnostic that maps the method onto the links of the reproduction engine it does and does not reach.*

#### In plain words

The method is the part of Gandhi that scripts skip, and skipping it is why so many answers reduce him to "change of heart". The method has six components and they are not all appeals to sentiment.

**Self-purification** targets the inner assent on which the practice depends — the acceptance, by those who practise exclusion, that the exclusion is right. **Truth-force** is a technique of contestation: it opposes the practice without converting the practitioner into an enemy, which is what allows reform to proceed without producing the defensive consolidation that repression usually generates. The **constructive programme** — education, sanitation, common service, village work — makes the reform a daily practice rather than a declared position; it is a social technology, not a sentiment. **Religious reinterpretation** attacks the sanction from inside the tradition by distinguishing living ethical religion from unjust accretion. **Non-violent persuasion** refuses class war. And **trusteeship and economic reform** aim at the material dependence through which caste power is actually exercised.

⚠️ Now run the method against the reproduction engine of Session 3 and the assessment writes itself. It reaches the ritual moralisation of distance directly, through temple entry and shared service. It reaches material dependence partly, through trusteeship and constructive work. It reaches the marriage circle only late. It does **not** reach the sacred sanction, because reinterpretation preserves the interpretive authority that sanctified the hierarchy. And it does not address representation as an autonomous claim at all — which is exactly the ground of the 1932 dispute.

#### The doctrine, exactly

✅ **The six instruments.** Change of heart and self-purification; non-violent truth-force (*satyāgraha*) against unjust practice; a constructive programme involving education, sanitation, common service and village work; religious reinterpretation from within Hindu life; non-violence and persuasion rather than class war; and trusteeship and economic reform to reduce dependence.

✅ **The four presuppositions.** Religious traditions contain resources for self-criticism; privileged persons can be morally transformed; means must embody the equal fellowship sought; and social unity should not be purchased through coercive hatred.

✅ **The achievement.** Gandhi brought caste humiliation into mass moral and political discourse, made caste Hindu responsibility central, linked social reform to everyday practice, and insisted that political freedom without internal moral reform was incomplete.

⚠️ **The diagnostic.** The method reaches the ritual moralisation of distance directly and material dependence partly; it reaches the marriage circle only in the later phase; it does not reach the sacred sanction, because reinterpretation preserves the interpretive authority; and it does not address representation as an autonomous claim.

#### Named evidence you can cite

- **The six-instrument method** ✅ — the owner's own list, which prevents the reduction of Gandhi's programme to "change of heart" and supplies the material for the method axis of the comparison matrix.
- **The four presuppositions** ✅ — the owner's statement of what must be true for the method to work; the fastest route to a critical evaluation, since each presupposition can be contested directly.
- **The means-embody-ends principle** ✅ — the presupposition that explains why coercive routes to equality are refused, and which is the strongest Gandhian reply to the charge of ineffectiveness.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING LINE — WRITE OR ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Gandhi's method is not an appeal to sentiment but a social technology of six instruments — self-purification, truth-force, constructive work, reinterpretation from within, non-violent persuasion and trusteeship — and it is best assessed by asking which link of caste's reproduction each instrument actually reaches.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

**inner assent removed** · **truth-force without enmity** · **constructive programme as daily practice** · **reinterpretation from within** · **refusal of class war** · **trusteeship reduces dependence** · **traditions contain self-criticism** · **means must embody ends** · **reaches ritual distance, not the sanction** · **representation not addressed**

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Gandhi's method is a structured programme of six instruments rather than an appeal to feeling, and the accurate criticism of it is not that it was sentimental but that it reaches some links of caste's reproduction and not others. **Named evidence.** The instruments are self-purification directed at the inner assent that sustains the practice; truth-force, which contests without producing an enemy; a constructive programme of education, sanitation, common service and village work that makes reform a daily practice; religious reinterpretation separating living ethical religion from unjust accretion; non-violent persuasion in place of class war; and trusteeship and economic reform aimed at the dependence through which caste power is exercised — resting on four presuppositions, of which the strongest is that means must embody the fellowship they seek. **Analysis.** Mapped onto the reproduction engine, the method reaches the ritual moralisation of distance directly, through temple entry and shared service; reaches material dependence partly, through trusteeship and constructive work; reaches the marriage circle only in the later phase; does not reach the sacred sanction, because reinterpretation preserves the interpretive authority that sanctified the hierarchy; and does not address representation as an autonomous claim at all. That map is the evaluation, and it is more precise than any general verdict on efficacy. **Qualification.** The achievement must be stated before the map is used against him: Gandhi brought caste humiliation into mass moral and political discourse, made the responsibility of the privileged central rather than incidental, tied reform to everyday conduct, and refused to treat political freedom as complete without internal moral reform — none of which any rival programme supplied, and the last of which Ambedkar independently affirms.

#### Objection → reply → residual limit

- **Objection.** ✅ The constructive programme is charity in a political costume. Sanitation work, village service and education undertaken by the privileged relieve symptoms and leave land, office and authority precisely where they were; the programme therefore functions to make the arrangement bearable rather than to end it.
- **Reply.** ⚠️ Two components resist the charge. The constructive programme is designed as **shared** practice, which attacks the ritual rule that certain labour pollutes those who perform it — a rule no transfer of resources touches. And **trusteeship with economic reform** is explicitly aimed at dependence, which is the enforcement mechanism rather than a symptom. The programme is not a substitute for redistribution; it is an attack on a different link.
- **Residual limit.** ❓ Whether trusteeship can in fact reduce dependence without enforceable redistribution is unresolved, and the owner does not settle it. What survives is a structural point rather than an empirical one: an arrangement in which the powerful are asked to hold property for others leaves the decision with the powerful, which is the same location-of-initiative problem that Session 9 identified in the argument itself.

#### Examiner traps on this session

37. Reducing the method to "change of heart". Six instruments are on record, and the reduction makes the criticism easy and unearned.
38. Omitting the method axis from a comparison. Method is where the two thinkers diverge most sharply and is the component examiners most often find missing.
39. Stating the criticism before the achievement. A critical evaluation that never says what the position secured is a denunciation, and the 2022 stem asks for the former.
40. Treating trusteeship as an economic doctrine to be expounded. It is named here as a caste-relevant instrument; the doctrine belongs to its own owner.

#### Mini recap

Six instruments: self-purification, truth-force, constructive programme, religious reinterpretation, non-violent persuasion, trusteeship and economic reform. Four presuppositions: traditions can self-criticise, the privileged can be transformed, means must embody ends, unity must not rest on hatred. One achievement: caste humiliation entered mass discourse, the responsibility of the privileged became central, reform became daily conduct, and political freedom was declared incomplete without moral reform. One diagnostic: reaches ritual distance and part of dependence, reaches marriage late, does not reach the sanction, does not address representation.

```text
  CLOSURE FLOW -- SESSION 10
  Gandhi's method -> SIX INSTRUMENTS resting on FOUR PRESUPPOSITIONS,
      not a single appeal to sentiment
      -> MECHANISM: self-purification attacks inner assent; shared
         service attacks the ritual rule that labour pollutes;
         trusteeship attacks dependence
      -> CONSEQUENCE: mapped onto the reproduction engine, the method
         reaches ritual distance and part of dependence, reaches marriage
         late, and reaches neither the sanction nor representation
      -> TRAP: reducing the method to change of heart; omitting method
         from a comparison; criticising before stating the achievement
      -> ANSWER USE: use the map as the evaluation -- it is more precise
         than any general verdict on efficacy
```

        v
  THE 2022 Q2(b) ROUTE -- "CRITICALLY EVALUATE"
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | THE OBJECTION-REPLY-RESIDUE LAYER IS THE ANSWER. TWO CHAINS FULLY   |
  | WORKED BEAT SIX CRITICISMS LISTED, AND A SCRIPT THAT ONLY LISTS     |
  | HAS ANSWERED "STATE THE CRITICISMS" -- A DIFFERENT QUESTION.        |
