   | WHAT THE FRANCHISE ENABLES          | WHAT IT DOES NOT DISSOLVE |
   | subordinated populations become     | candidate selection,      |
   | political CONSTITUENCIES; voice,    | patronage, symbolic       |
   | bargaining, representation and      | mobilisation, social      |
   | policy attention become available   | blocs, unequal STATUS     |
   +-------------------------------------+---------------------------+
        |
        v
THE NORMATIVE SEPARATION THAT MUST BE STATED -- NOT ALL CASTE POLITICS IS ONE
  +--------------+-------------------------------+----------------------+
  | ASSERTION    | seeks equality, voice and     | normatively           |
  |              | equal citizenship             | DEFENSIBLE            |
  +--------------+-------------------------------+----------------------+
  | SUPREMACY    | seeks inherited privilege and | normatively           |
  |              | preserved rank                | INDEFENSIBLE          |
  +--------------+-------------------------------+----------------------+
  | ELECTORAL    | ordinarily contains elements  | JUDGE BY PROGRAMME    |
  | MOBILISATION | of both                       | AND EFFECT, not label |
  +--------------+-------------------------------+----------------------+
        |
        v
AMBEDKAR'S CONTRADICTION -- THE CLOSING MOVE OF ALMOST EVERY 20-MARKER
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | ONE PERSON ONE VOTE AND ONE VOTE ONE VALUE, SET AGAINST A SOCIAL    |
  | ORDER OF GRADED WORTH, IS A CONTRADICTION A POLITY CANNOT           |
  | INDEFINITELY BEAR. POLITICAL DEMOCRACY REQUIRES SOCIAL DEMOCRACY.   |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
2018 Q1(c) BRIDGE -> caste survives alternate identities because the FRANCHISE
  reaches the citizen while ENDOGAMY, RITUAL RANK AND DEPENDENCE reach the
  household. Gandhi's answer is transformed social conduct; the residue is
  that conduct without rights leaves the change revocable.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 8/22: The four dimensions of caste injustice and their distinct remedies
DO NOT TREAT THESE AS SUBSTITUTES. EACH ANSWERS A DIFFERENT MECHANISM.
  +----------------+------------------------------+---------------------+
  | DIMENSION      | THE CASTE MECHANISM          | THE REQUIRED        |
  |                |                              | RESPONSE            |
  +----------------+------------------------------+---------------------+
  | REDISTRIBUTION | unequal land, labour,        | resources and       |
  |                | education, networks, security| institutional access|
  +----------------+------------------------------+---------------------+
  | RECOGNITION /  | stigma, purity/pollution,    | repudiation of      |
  | STATUS         | humiliation, graded worth    | inherited rank      |
  +----------------+------------------------------+---------------------+
  | REPRESENTATION | others speak for the group;  | autonomous voice    |
  |                | candidate and office access  | and political       |
  |                | controlled elsewhere         | safeguards          |
  +----------------+------------------------------+---------------------+
  | RELIGION       | hierarchy presented as       | freedom of          |
  |                | sacred authority             | conscience PLUS no  |
  |                |                              | civic disability    |
  +----------------+------------------------------+---------------------+
  | GENDER         | endogamy regulates marriage, | anti-caste and      |
  |                | sexuality and property       | gender analysis     |
  |                |                              | JOINTLY structured  |
  +----------------+------------------------------+---------------------+
  | CLASS          | exploitation interacts with, | material analysis   |
  |                | but does not exhaust, status | WITHOUT reduction   |
  |                | closure                      | of caste to class   |
  +----------------+------------------------------+---------------------+
        |
        v
THE TWO FAILURE MODES THIS GRID NAMES
  IDENTITY WITHOUT REDISTRIBUTION -> material power is left intact
  REDISTRIBUTION WITHOUT RECOGNITION -> humiliation is preserved
TRAP -> Dalit women's position is NOT the arithmetic sum of caste plus
  gender analysed separately; endogamy makes the two structurally joint.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 9/22: Gandhi periodised -- three phases and the residue that survives
NEVER FLATTEN GANDHI INTO ONE TIMELESS POSITION. THE PERIODISATION IS ITSELF
A MARK-BEARING MOVE, AND IT IS THE FIRST THING A 2022 OR 2025 STEM REWARDS.
  P1 . EARLIER PHASE
       distinguishes an IDEALISED HEREDITARY DUTY-ORDER (VARNA) from caste
       hierarchy; presents it as non-competitive, without superiority,
       a scheme of duties rather than of ranks
              |
              v
  P2 . ANTI-UNTOUCHABILITY CAMPAIGN
       condemns exclusion as sin; temple entry; shared sanitation work and
       common service; the term "HARIJAN" is introduced -- LATER REJECTED BY
       MANY DALIT THINKERS AS PATERNALISTIC
              |
              v
  P3 . LATER MOVEMENT
       increasingly critical of birth-based caste barriers; increasingly
       supportive of INTER-CASTE MARRIAGE
              |
              v
  RESIDUE -> AN IDEALISED DUTY-ORDER CAN STILL ALLOCATE BY BIRTH EVEN AFTER
        EXPLICIT RANKING IS DENIED. THAT IS A GENUINE THEORETICAL LIABILITY
        AND IT IS NOT CANCELLED BY THE LATER POSITION.
        |
        v
PERIODISATION CONTROL -- THE QUALIFICATION THAT MUST BE WRITTEN
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | THIS IS A TRAJECTORY RECONSTRUCTED ACROSS DECADES OF WRITING AND    |
  | PRACTICE. IT IS NOT A CLAIM THAT ONE DATE OR ONE SENTENCE CLEANLY   |
  | REPLACED EVERY EARLIER COMMITMENT. STATE THE LATER OPPOSITION TO    |
  | BIRTH BARRIERS TOGETHER WITH -- NOT INSTEAD OF -- THE EARLIER       |
  | LIABILITY.                                                          |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
TERM CONTROL -> "Harijan" is GANDHI'S HISTORICAL USAGE, contested at the time
  and since. It is never the narrator's neutral term.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 10/22: Gandhi's argument against untouchability -- five steps
THE DOCTRINE, STATED AS AN ARGUMENT RATHER THAN AS A SENTIMENT
  1. ALL LIFE PARTICIPATES IN A MORAL-SPIRITUAL UNITY, so persons are of
     equal spiritual worth whatever their birth-group.
                 |
                 v
  2. DEGRADING ANOTHER PERSON VIOLATES NON-VIOLENCE (AHIMSA) AND TRUTH.
     Untouchability is therefore not merely unkind; it is a breach of the
     two commitments on which the whole Gandhian ethic rests.
                 |
                 v
  3. INHERITED EXCLUSION CORRUPTS BOTH PARTIES -- the excluded and the caste
     Hindu conscience that practises the exclusion.
                 |
                 v
  4. THEREFORE REFORM MUST INVOLVE REPENTANCE AND CHANGED CONDUCT BY THE
     PRIVILEGED. Responsibility is placed on the dominant, not on the victim.
                 |
                 v
  5. LEGAL PROHIBITION IS NECESSARY BUT CANNOT BY ITSELF GENERATE FELLOWSHIP.
     Law can forbid an act; it cannot manufacture regard.
        |
        v
THE STRONGEST FORM OF THE GANDHIAN CLAIM -- WORTH QUOTING AS A POSITION
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | UNTOUCHABILITY IS A SIN AGAINST HUMANITY AND AGAINST RELIGION       |
  | ITSELF, TO BE EXPIATED BY THE PRACTISING COMMUNITY THROUGH PENANCE, |
  | SERVICE AND SELF-PURIFICATION. NO RELIGIOUS SANCTION CAN ATTACH TO  |
  | THE DEGRADATION OF A HUMAN BEING.                                   |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
LIMIT, STATED IMMEDIATELY -> This places the initiative with the dominant
  group's conscience. That is precisely Ambedkar's central objection, and an
  answer that states the argument without the limit has written half of it.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 11/22: Gandhi's method, presuppositions and achievement
METHOD IS WHERE GANDHI AND AMBEDKAR MOST SHARPLY DIVERGE, AND IT IS THE
COMPONENT MOST ROUTINELY OMITTED FROM SCRIPTS.
  +---------------------------+---------------------------------------+
  | INSTRUMENT                | WHAT IT IS SUPPOSED TO DO             |
  +---------------------------+---------------------------------------+
  | CHANGE OF HEART AND       | remove the inner assent on which the  |
  | SELF-PURIFICATION         | practice depends                      |
  +---------------------------+---------------------------------------+
  | TRUTH-FORCE (SATYAGRAHA)  | contest unjust practice without        |
  |                           | converting the opponent into an enemy |
  +---------------------------+---------------------------------------+
  | CONSTRUCTIVE PROGRAMME    | education, sanitation, common service  |
  |                           | and village work as DAILY PRACTICE    |
  +---------------------------+---------------------------------------+
  | RELIGIOUS REINTERPRETATION| separate living ethical religion from |
  |                           | unjust accretion, FROM WITHIN         |
  +---------------------------+---------------------------------------+
  | NON-VIOLENT PERSUASION    | refuse class war and coercive hatred  |
  +---------------------------+---------------------------------------+
  | TRUSTEESHIP AND ECONOMIC  | reduce the DEPENDENCE through which   |
  | REFORM                    | caste power is actually exercised     |
  +---------------------------+---------------------------------------+
        |
        v
PRESUPPOSITIONS -> traditions contain resources for self-criticism | the
  privileged can be morally transformed | means must EMBODY the fellowship
  sought | social unity must not be purchased through coercive hatred
        |
        v
ACHIEVEMENT, STATED FAIRLY BEFORE THE CRITIQUE
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | GANDHI BROUGHT CASTE HUMILIATION INTO MASS MORAL AND POLITICAL      |
  | DISCOURSE, MADE CASTE HINDU RESPONSIBILITY CENTRAL, TIED REFORM TO  |
  | EVERYDAY PRACTICE, AND INSISTED THAT POLITICAL FREEDOM WITHOUT      |
  | INTERNAL MORAL REFORM WAS INCOMPLETE.                               |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 12/22: Four objections to Gandhi, four replies, four residues
GIVE EACH OBJECTION AT ITS STRONGEST. A REPLY TO A WEAK OBJECTION EARNS
NOTHING, AND THE RESIDUE IS WHERE THE CRITICAL MARK ACTUALLY SITS.
  +---------------+-------------------------+-------------------------+
  | OBJECTION     | GANDHIAN REPLY          | RESIDUE THAT SURVIVES   |
  +---------------+-------------------------+-------------------------+
  | PATERNALISM   | the moral responsibility| the objection STANDS    |
  | privileged    | of oppressors is        | unless oppressed        |
  | reformers     | indispensable and cannot| communities hold        |
  | speak for the | be delegated            | AUTONOMOUS voice and    |
  | oppressed and |                         | leadership; renaming is |
  | rename them   |                         | not transfer of power   |
  +---------------+-------------------------+-------------------------+
  | STRUCTURAL    | the constructive        | ENFORCEABLE RIGHTS are  |
  | heart-change  | programme and satyagraha| still required; justice |
  | leaves        | are social practices,   | that depends on         |
  | property,     | not private sentiment   | benevolence is          |
  | office and    |                         | revocable               |
  | exclusion     |                         |                         |
  | untouched     |                         |                         |
  +---------------+-------------------------+-------------------------+
  | VARNA         | the later trajectory    | the EARLY DEFENCES      |
  | hereditary    | weakens birth hierarchy | remain a genuine        |
  | duty          | and supports inter-caste| theoretical liability   |
  | reproduces    | marriage                | and cannot be argued    |
  | caste even    |                         | away by chronology      |
  | without rank  |                         |                         |
  +---------------+-------------------------+-------------------------+
  | RELIGIOUS     | living ethical religion | AMBEDKAR REPLIES that   |
  | REFORM        | is distinguishable from | the AUTHORITY STRUCTURE |
  | reinterpreting| unjust accretion        | itself must be broken,  |
  | scripture     |                         | not reinterpreted       |
  | preserves the |                         |                         |
  | authority     |                         |                         |
  +---------------+-------------------------+-------------------------+
2022 Q2(b) ROUTE -> "Critically evaluate" means the objection-reply-residue
  layer IS the answer. Two chains, fully worked, beat six listed criticisms.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 13/22: Ambedkar I -- endogamy as the mechanism (Castes in India, 1916)
THE ORIGIN QUESTION IS DISPUTED; THE MECHANISM IS THE LOAD-BEARING CLAIM.
USE THE MECHANISM, NOT THE HISTORICAL GENESIS, AS THE ANCHOR.
  STEP 1 -> A CASTE MUST PRESERVE A CLOSED MARRIAGE CIRCLE. Endogamy is not
     one feature among many; it is the condition of the group's existence.
              |
              v
  STEP 2 -> DEMOGRAPHIC IMBALANCE INSIDE THE GROUP THREATENS THAT CLOSURE.
     A surplus of unmarried men or women opens the boundary.
              |
              v
  STEP 3 -> SOCIAL PRACTICES REGULATING WIDOWS, WIDOWERS AND MARRIAGE ARISE
     TO MAINTAIN THE CIRCLE. The control of women is structurally required.
              |
              v
  STEP 4 -> THEREFORE CASTE IS REPRODUCED THROUGH THE CONTROL OF SEXUALITY
     AND FAMILY, NOT ONLY THROUGH OCCUPATION.
        |
        v
THE BRIDGE TO GENDER -- WRITE IT, IT IS A DISTINGUISHING SENTENCE
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | CASTE CONTINUITY DEPENDS ON REGULATING WOMEN'S MARRIAGE AND         |
  | SEXUALITY. ANTI-CASTE AND GENDER ANALYSIS ARE THEREFORE JOINTLY     |
  | STRUCTURED RATHER THAN SEPARATELY ADDED. THE FULL GENDER DOCTRINE   |
  | IS ROUTED TO ITS OWN OWNER AND NEVER DEVELOPED HERE.                |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |
        v
WHY THIS BEATS EVERY DESCRIPTIVE OPENING
  a script that opens "caste is a hereditary system of social stratification"
  has said nothing an examiner cannot supply. A script that opens with the
  CLOSED MARRIAGE CIRCLE has already named the reproduction mechanism, and
  every subsequent remedy can be tested against it.
LIMIT -> the 1916 account of ORIGIN is one hypothesis among several. Do not
  assert it as settled history; assert the mechanism.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 14/22: Ambedkar II -- Annihilation of Caste (1936), the five moves
THE TARGET IS THE SANCTION, NOT THE PRACTICE. REFORM OF UNTOUCHABILITY ALONE
IS EXPLICITLY REJECTED AS INSUFFICIENT.
  1. CASTE IS SUSTAINED BY BELIEF IN THE SACRED AUTHORITY OF RULES THAT RANK
     AND SEPARATE. The system rests on a sanctified belief-system.
                 |
                 v
  2. ISOLATED REFORMS LEAVE THAT NORMATIVE FOUNDATION INTACT, which is why
     the hierarchy survives every reform of its outward practice.
                 |
                 v
  3. INTER-DINING ALONE DOES NOT RELIABLY BREAK THE SYSTEM. A shared meal
     does not open the marriage circle.
                 |
                 v
  4. INTER-CASTE MARRIAGE ATTACKS ENDOGAMY AND CREATES SOCIAL KINSHIP. It
     reaches the mechanism identified in 1916.
                 |
                 v
  5. ANNIHILATION THEREFORE REQUIRES REJECTION OR RADICAL RECONSTRUCTION OF
     SCRIPTURAL AUTHORITY, SOCIAL RIGHTS AND MATERIAL RELATIONS.
        |
        v
CASTE AS ANTI-SOCIAL AND ANTI-NATIONAL
  +--------------------------------+---------------------------------+
  | CULTURAL PLURALITY              | CASTE                          |
  | interaction among EQUAL groups; | RANKED SEPARATION; loyalty     |
  | difference without ranking      | narrowed to the group; public  |
  |                                 | spirit and common action       |
  |                                 | obstructed                     |
  +---------------------------------+--------------------------------+
  CASTE IS NOT DIVERSITY. IT IS RANKED SEPARATION -- SAY THIS EXPLICITLY.
        |
        v
2023 Q3(b) ROUTE -> "social and political significance of annihilation":
  SOCIAL = the marriage circle, association and the valuation of persons;
  POLITICAL = the sanction, representation and the conditions of democracy.
  A script that treats annihilation as a slogan for "removing caste feeling"
  has lost both halves.
LIMIT, CONCEDED -> The state's competence to alter religious BELIEF is
  limited. That limit is exactly why conversion and constitutional morality
  enter the programme.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 15/22: Ambedkar III -- liberty, equality and fraternity as one triad
NOT THREE SEPARATE IDEALS. AN INSEPARABLE SOCIAL-DEMOCRATIC TRIAD IN WHICH
EACH TERM FAILS WITHOUT THE OTHER TWO.
   LIBERTY WITHOUT EQUALITY -> permits DOMINATION by the already powerful
   EQUALITY WITHOUT LIBERTY -> suppresses INDIVIDUALITY
   BOTH WITHOUT FRATERNITY  -> lack the SOCIAL DISPOSITION to recognise
                               others as equals at all
        |
        v
FRATERNITY, DEFINED AGAINST THE COMMONEST MISREADING
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | FRATERNITY IS NOT SENTIMENTALITY OR GOODWILL. IT IS THE SOCIAL      |
  | BASIS OF DEMOCRACY: A SENSE OF COMMON ASSOCIATED LIFE AMONG SOCIAL  |
  | EQUALS, SUPPORTED BY RIGHTS AND INSTITUTIONS. IT IS THE LEAST       |
  | ATTENDED AND MOST NECESSARY OF THE THREE, AND NO QUOTA PRODUCES IT. |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
        |
        v
SOCIAL DEMOCRACY -- THE CONCEPT THAT CLOSES ALMOST EVERY 20-MARKER
  political democracy = periodic voting and formal equal citizenship
  SOCIAL DEMOCRACY   = liberty, equality and fraternity AS A WAY OF LIFE;
                       democracy as a MODE OF ASSOCIATED LIVING
  A POLITICAL DEMOCRACY RESTING ON A SOCIAL ORDER OF GRADED INEQUALITY IS
  UNSTABLE. THAT INSTABILITY, NOT A SHORTAGE OF GOODWILL, IS THE DIAGNOSIS.
        |
        v
CONSTITUTIONAL MORALITY -- WHY LEGAL ABOLITION IS NOT ENOUGH
  the FORM of administration can be perverted WITHOUT ALTERING THE TEXT;
  therefore a DIFFUSED PUBLIC COMMITMENT to constitutional norms must be
  cultivated. A safeguard is inert without the disposition that sustains it.
LIMIT -> constitutional morality names a CONDITION without specifying how it
  is produced. Concede this; it is Ambedkar's own diagnosis, not a defeat.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 16/22: Ambedkar IV -- rights, representation and the argument for power
THE ARGUMENT FROM SOCIAL POWER, IN FIVE MOVES
  1. CASTE IS ENFORCED THROUGH SOCIAL POWER, not merely private prejudice.
  2. VICTIMS THEREFORE NEED RIGHTS AND REMEDIES INDEPENDENT OF DOMINANT
     GOODWILL. A benefit that can be withdrawn is not a right.
  3. POLITICAL REPRESENTATION ENABLES SUBORDINATED GROUPS TO SPEAK FOR
     THEMSELVES rather than be spoken for.
  4. CONSTITUTIONAL MORALITY RESTRAINS INHERITED SOCIAL AUTHORITY, including
     the authority of a social majority.
  5. DEMOCRACY MUST BECOME A MODE OF ASSOCIATED LIVING, not only periodic
     voting.
        |
        v
EDUCATE -- AGITATE -- ORGANISE: A POWER PROGRAMME, NOT SELF-HELP
  +-------------+------------------------------------------------------+
  | EDUCATE     | produces CRITICAL SELF-RESPECT and enlarged agency    |
  | AGITATE     | CONTESTS public injustice through collective action  |
  | ORGANISE    | builds AUTONOMOUS COLLECTIVE POWER                    |
  +-------------+------------------------------------------------------+
  READ AS AN INTEGRATED PROGRAMME OF EMANCIPATION AND REPRESENTATION.
  NEVER as a motivational slogan detached from rights and material power.
        |
        v
CONVERSION AND BUDDHISM AS A NEW VEHICLE (NAVAYANA), 1956
  emancipation required DEPARTURE from a religious-social order judged
  inseparable from caste authority; the conversion expresses an ETHICAL
  RECONSTRUCTION around reason, compassion, equality and fraternity
  IT IS BOTH personal freedom of conscience AND collective social critique.
  IT IS NOT a change of ritual label, and it PLACES THE REMEDY OUTSIDE THE
  STATE ALTOGETHER.
TRAP -> Do not develop classical Buddhist doctrine here; it is another
  owner's property. Name Navayana's ethical-social reconstruction and stop.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 17/22: Ambedkar beyond reservation, and the Marx comparison
THE MOST DAMAGING SINGLE ERROR ON THIS CLAUSE IS TO PRESENT AMBEDKAR AS A
THEORIST OF RESERVATION. RESERVATION IS A SAFEGUARD, AND BY HIS OWN ACCOUNT
AN INSUFFICIENT ONE.
  +----------------------+----------------------------------------------+
  | ELEMENT              | WHY IT EXCEEDS ANY QUOTA                     |
  +----------------------+----------------------------------------------+
  | ANNIHILATION         | reservation redistributes positions inside a |
  |                      | hierarchy it does not dissolve               |
  +----------------------+----------------------------------------------+
  | ENDOGAMY             | no quota touches the marriage circle          |
  +----------------------+----------------------------------------------+
  | CONSTITUTIONAL       | a legal safeguard is inert without the       |
  | MORALITY             | disposition that sustains it                 |
  +----------------------+----------------------------------------------+
  | SOCIAL DEMOCRACY     | representation without it leaves the         |
  |                      | one-vote-one-value contradiction standing    |
  +----------------------+----------------------------------------------+
  | FRATERNITY           | cannot be legislated; no quota produces it   |
  +----------------------+----------------------------------------------+
  | EDUCATE, AGITATE,    | reservation without organised capacity is a  |
  | ORGANISE             | benefit RECEIVED rather than power HELD      |
  +----------------------+----------------------------------------------+
  | CONVERSION           | places the remedy outside the state entirely |
  +----------------------+----------------------------------------------+
  | STATE-DIRECTED       | political safeguards do not touch economic   |
  | ECONOMIC POWER       | dependence on dominant-caste landowners and  |
  |                      | employers (his States and Minorities         |
  |                      | proposals)                                   |
  +----------------------+----------------------------------------------+
  ONE-LINE THESIS -> RESERVATION ADDRESSES ACCESS TO POSITIONS; ANNIHILATION
  ADDRESSES THE VALUATION OF PERSONS, AND ONLY THE SECOND PRODUCES SOCIAL
  DEMOCRACY.
        |
        v
AMBEDKAR AND MARX -- STATE IT PRECISELY, NEITHER FUSED NOR OPPOSED
  +-------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | AXIS        | MARXIAN EMPHASIS          | AMBEDKARITE EMPHASIS     |
  +-------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | HIERARCHY   | ownership and class       | caste status, endogamy,  |
  |             | relation                  | religion AND class       |
  +-------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | AGENCY      | the proletarian class     | oppressed castes through |
  |             |                           | rights and organisation  |
  +-------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | METHOD      | revolutionary             | constitutional democracy |
  |             | transformation            | plus social struggle     |
  +-------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | IDEAL       | classless society         | social democracy of      |
  |             |                           | liberty, equality,       |
  |             |                           | fraternity               |
  +-------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  THE PRECISE POINT -> he ACCEPTS that economic power matters and that the
  state must act on property, but DENIES that caste is a form of class. An
  economic revolution that leaves the valuation of persons intact reproduces
  hierarchy in a new form.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 18/22: Three objections to Ambedkar, three replies, three residues
  +---------------+--------------------------+--------------------------+
  | OBJECTION     | REPLY                    | RESIDUE                  |
  +---------------+--------------------------+--------------------------+
  | LEGALISM      | he NEVER relies on law   | implementation, evasion  |
  | law cannot    | alone: law changes power,| and everyday caste       |
  | abolish social| protects action and      | consciousness remain     |
  | attitudes     | enables social movements | live problems            |
  +---------------+--------------------------+--------------------------+
  | FRAGMENTATION | unity built on the       | elite capture and        |
  | separate      | SILENCE of the oppressed | permanent bloc politics  |
  | organisation  | is domination; equal     | are genuine risks;       |
  | divides       | representation is a      | organisation needs       |
  | national unity| CONDITION of legitimate  | internal democratic      |
  |               | common life              | accountability           |
  +---------------+--------------------------+--------------------------+
  | RELIGIOUS     | the target is authority  | believers may experience |
  | HOSTILITY     | INSOFAR AS it sanctifies | the critique as hostile; |
  | rejecting     | graded inequality;       | the distinction between  |
  | scriptural    | freedom of belief cannot | doctrine and civic       |
  | authority     | entail the power to      | disability must be       |
  | attacks       | impose CIVIC DISABILITY  | argued, not assumed      |
  | believers     |                          |                          |
  +---------------+--------------------------+--------------------------+
        |
        v
TWO FURTHER STANDARD CRITICISMS, WITH THEIR REPLIES
  "AMBEDKAR OVERSTATES SCRIPTURAL CAUSATION" -> he also analyses endogamy,
     power, occupation and representation. RESIDUE: regional diversity of
     caste is not fully captured by any single causal story.
  "INTER-CASTE MARRIAGE IS INSUFFICIENT" -> it attacks endogamy but needs
     economic, legal and cultural change. RESIDUE: coercion and family
     violence against couples are real and unresolved by exhortation.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 19/22: The comparison matrix -- run the axes, never two biographies
THE SINGLE COMMONEST WAY TO LOSE MARKS ON THE 2019 AND 2025 STEMS IS TO
WRITE TWO SEQUENTIAL MINI-ESSAYS. FIX THE AXES AND RUN BOTH DOWN EACH.
  +--------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | AXIS         | GANDHI                    | AMBEDKAR                 |
  +--------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | PRIMARY EVIL | untouchability and moral  | CASTE ITSELF as graded   |
  |              | degradation; later, wider | inequality               |
  |              | caste barriers            |                          |
  +--------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | HEREDITARY   | idealised duty/division   | birth-based division is  |
  | DUTY (VARNA) | WITHOUT rank              | INTRINSICALLY unjust     |
  +--------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | ROOT         | corrupted religion,       | endogamy, scripture,     |
  | MECHANISM    | prejudice, lost           | social closure and power |
  |              | conscience                |                          |
  +--------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | AGENT OF     | transformed caste Hindu   | the oppressed people's   |
  | REFORM       | conscience plus oppressed | AUTONOMOUS organisation  |
  |              | participation             | and representation       |
  +--------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | METHOD       | non-violence, self-       | rights, law, education,  |
  |              | purification, constructive| organisation,            |
  |              | work, reform              | representation, marriage |
  +--------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | RELIGION     | reform FROM WITHIN        | reject caste-sanctioning |
  |              |                           | authority; conversion    |
  +--------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | DEMOCRACY    | ethical fellowship and    | constitutional morality  |
  |              | self-rule (SWARAJ)        | and social democracy     |
  +--------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
  | MAIN RISK    | paternalism and structural| legal and institutional  |
  |              | insufficiency             | reform without social    |
  |              |                           | fraternity               |
  +--------------+---------------------------+--------------------------+
        |
        v
CONVERGENCE WITHOUT FALSE EQUIVALENCE
  +-------------------------------+---------------------------------+
  | GENUINE CONVERGENCE           | IRREDUCIBLE DISAGREEMENT        |
  +-------------------------------+---------------------------------+
  | untouchability is morally     | is caste itself, or only its    |
  | indefensible                  | degraded form, the disease?     |
  | equal human worth and social  | can a hereditary duty-order be  |
  | fellowship matter             | purified at all?                |
  | political freedom without     | can reform rely on dominant-    |
  | social reform is incomplete   | caste conscience?               |
  | institutions AND conduct must | do oppressed groups require     |
  | both change                   | AUTONOMOUS electoral safeguards?|
  | violence cannot found stable  | is EXIT from caste-sanctioning  |
  | equality                      | religion necessary?             |
  +-------------------------------+---------------------------------+
  CONTROL -> convergence identifies COMMON ENDS. It does not equalise
  diagnostic adequacy or political power, and a synthesis is defensible only
  when moral conversion SUPPORTS autonomous rights rather than postponing them.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 20/22: Poona Pact 1932, secular democracy, village and modernity
INTERPRET THE PACT. DO NOT NARRATE IT, AND DO NOT ASSERT FIGURES OR
NEGOTIATING DETAIL BEYOND WHAT THE OWNER STATES.
  THE COMMUNAL AWARD (1932) provided SEPARATE ELECTORAL ARRANGEMENTS for the
  Depressed Classes
              |
              v
  GANDHI OPPOSED separate electorates and began a FAST
              |
              v
  NEGOTIATIONS WITH AMBEDKAR AND OTHER LEADERS produced the POONA PACT (1932),
  substituting RESERVED SEATS IN JOINT ELECTORATES with a PRIMARY-ELECTION
  MECHANISM, and INCREASING the number of reserved seats
              |
              v
  THE PHILOSOPHICAL CONFLICT, WHICH IS THE ACTUAL ANSWER
  +--------------------------------+---------------------------------+
  | GANDHI FEARED                  | AMBEDKAR FEARED                 |
  | permanent political separation | that joint electorates would    |
  | and the fragmentation of Hindu | leave Depressed Class            |
  | society                        | representatives DEPENDENT ON    |
  |                                | DOMINANT-CASTE VOTES            |
  +--------------------------------+---------------------------------+
  THE ISSUE WAS NOT SEAT NUMBERS. IT WAS WHETHER A SUBORDINATED GROUP
  REQUIRES AUTONOMOUS POLITICAL VOICE AGAINST A SOCIAL MAJORITY -- AND WHO
  MAY CHOOSE ITS REPRESENTATIVES.
  COERCION CONTROL -> the Pact was a COMPROMISE REACHED UNDER MORALLY AND
  POLITICALLY COERCIVE CIRCUMSTANCES. Never narrate it as harmonious consensus.
        |
        v
SECULAR DEMOCRACY -- THE 2019 Q4(a) AXIS
  GANDHI grounds secular fellowship in EQUAL REGARD FOR RELIGIONS,
     non-violence and conscience -> AN ETHIC OF COEXISTENCE
  AMBEDKAR grounds democracy in CONSTITUTIONAL MORALITY, equal citizenship
     and freedom from religiously sanctioned hierarchy -> AN INSTITUTIONAL
     TEST: no religious community may convert internal doctrine into CIVIC
     INFERIORITY
        |
        v
VILLAGE, COMMUNITY AND MODERNITY -- NOT "TRADITION VERSUS MODERNITY"
  GANDHI: decentralised community as a possible school of responsibility,
     meaningful labour and self-rule
  AMBEDKAR: the ACTUALLY EXISTING village can concentrate caste authority,
     dependency and exclusion
  THE TEST -> does scale, technology or community ENLARGE mobility, equal
  status, constitutional rights and EXIT from inherited occupation?
  CENTRALISED MODERN INSTITUTIONS CAN REPRODUCE CASTE; LOCAL COMMUNITY CAN
  DO SO AS WELL. NEITHER SCALE IS EMANCIPATORY WITHOUT ANTI-CASTE POWER.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 21/22: Affirmative action -- four grounds, the objection, the record
NAMING THE JUSTIFICATION IS THE MARK-BEARING MOVE. RUNNING ALL FOUR TOGETHER
LETS AN OBJECTION THAT APPLIES TO ONLY ONE OF THEM DEFEAT THE WHOLE ANSWER.
  +----------------+--------------------------+------------------------+
  | GROUND         | CORE CLAIM               | TERMINATES WHEN        |
  +----------------+--------------------------+------------------------+
  | COMPENSATORY   | positive measures rectify| the transmitted        |

  |                | identifiable, legally    | operates               |

  |                | whose effects transmit   |                        |
  +----------------+--------------------------+------------------------+
  | DISTRIBUTIVE / | formal equality from     | disadvantage in access |
  | EQUAL          | today ratifies an unequal| is removed             |
  | OPPORTUNITY    | starting line            |                        |
  +----------------+--------------------------+------------------------+

  |                | and publicly repudiates  | social life            |

  +----------------+--------------------------+------------------------+
  THE DEFENSIBLE JOIN -> COMPENSATORY IN GROUND, DISTRIBUTIVE IN MECHANISM,
  REPRESENTATIONAL IN INSTITUTIONAL JUSTIFICATION, ANTI-DOMINATION IN AIM.

        v
REVERSE DISCRIMINATION -- OBJECTION AT ITS STRONGEST, THEN FIVE REPLIES
  OBJECTION: persons must be treated as individuals; selection should track
  qualification; reservation allocates by ascriptive membership; the excluded
  individual is personally innocent; so a fresh injustice is inflicted using
  the very logic -- allocation by birth-group -- that is being condemned.
  R1 SYMMETRY ...... the unreserved baseline is not neutral; qualifications
       are themselves unequally producible. Two group-inflected systems, one
       candid about it.
  R2 MERIT AS PROXY  merit is FUNCTIONAL capacity for the role; examination
       performance is a PROXY whose reliability varies with coaching,
       nutrition, language and schooling. CONTEST THE MEASURE, NOT THE VALUE.
  R3 ADVANTAGE, NOT GUILT  no culpability is attributed; the claim is that
       some are UNJUSTLY ADVANTAGED by a continuing distribution.
  R4 GROUP HARM, GROUP REMEDY  a group-inflicted, group-transmitted
       disadvantage cannot be reached by a purely individualised remedy.
  R5 THE RESIDUE, CONCEDED  (i) OVER-INCLUSION of the already-advantaged
       within the beneficiary group; (ii) the cost falls UNEVENLY, near the
       margin rather than on the most advantaged. NAME BOTH.
  STIGMA PAIR -> objection: preference stigmatises beneficiaries. Reply: the
       stigma tracks the assumption that the unreserved baseline measured
       worth; representation is claimed as RIGHT AND POWER, not concession.
        |
        v
THE INDIAN RECORD -- STATE THE TYPE, THE DATE AND THE LIMIT OF EACH
  ARTICLE 17 ......... constitutional rule ABOLISHING untouchability and
       forbidding its practice. NOT evidence the practice has disappeared.
  SC/ST (PREVENTION OF ATROCITIES) ACT, 1989, in force 1990 ... an ENACTED
       penal and protective STATUTE.
  MANDAL COMMISSION ... the Second Backward Classes Commission, appointed
       1979, reported 1980. A COMMISSION REPORT RECOMMENDS; IT DOES NOT
       ENACT. Government action on OBC reservation in central services
       followed in 1990.
  INDRA SAWHNEY v. UNION OF INDIA (1992) ... a nine-judge SUPREME COURT
       JUDGMENT: upheld OBC reservation in central services under Article
       16(4); directed EXCLUSION OF THE CREAMY LAYER; held reservation should
       ORDINARILY NOT EXCEED 50 PER CENT save in extraordinary situations.
       ATTRIBUTE NOTHING FURTHER TO IT.
  CONSTITUTION (103rd AMENDMENT) ACT, 2019 ... an ENABLING AMENDMENT
       inserting ARTICLES 15(6) AND 16(6), permitting special provision
       including reservation UP TO TEN PER CENT for economically weaker
       sections OTHER THAN the classes already covered, upheld by a majority
       in JANHIT ABHIYAN v. UNION OF INDIA (2022).
  CREAMY LAYER ....... the INTERNAL FILTER answering over-inclusion; it
       concedes the group marker is a PROXY. COST: it strengthens the
       distributive ground and WEAKENS the purely representational one.
  THE 2019 PHILOSOPHICAL POINT -> an ECONOMIC criterion inside a framework
       built on SOCIAL AND EDUCATIONAL backwardness reopens the foundational
       question: is the wrong DEPRIVATION or ASCRIPTIVE EXCLUSION?
  FOUR-STAGE CAUTION -> ENACTMENT, NOTIFICATION, ENFORCEMENT AND SOCIAL
       TRANSFORMATION ARE FOUR DIFFERENT STAGES. NONE MAY BE INFERRED FROM
       ANOTHER, AND NO INSTRUMENT PROVES A PHILOSOPHICAL POSITION.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 22/22: Answer spine, the twenty traps and the qualified verdict
THE DIRECTIVE FIXES THE STRUCTURE -- DECODE IT BEFORE WRITING A WORD
  EXPLAIN / ELUCIDATE ...... doctrine -> mechanism -> key distinction ->
       example. FAILURE: narrating a life or the Pact as a story.
  DISCUSS .................. position -> rival -> objection -> reply ->
       verdict. FAILURE: two biographies side by side.
  CRITICALLY EXAMINE ....... TWO objection-reply chains, each ending in a
       RESIDUE. The critical layer IS the answer. FAILURE: denunciation.
  COMPARE / CONTRAST ....... fix 5-6 axes, run both down each, then
       adjudicate. FAILURE: sequential mini-essays.
  IS X JUSTIFIED? .......... NAME the ground first, then defend, then meet
       reverse discrimination. FAILURE: justification with no ground.
  HOW FAR / TO WHAT EXTENT . a DEGREE judgment is compulsory: conditions of
       holding and of failing. FAILURE: evasive balance.
        |
        v
THE FOUR-MOVE SPINE THAT FITS EVERY OWNED STEM
  1. DEFINE the exact mechanism or thinker-axis -- graded, birth-based social
     closure sustained by endogamy. Never "social stratification".
  2. DISTINGUISH -- varna/jati/caste; reform/annihilation; law/social change;
     assertion/supremacy; enactment/transformation.
  3. ARGUE -- named anchor -> analysis -> objection -> reply -> RESIDUE.
  4. CONCLUDE with a GRADED verdict, never a social-justice slogan.
        |
        v
THE TWENTY TRAPS, COMPRESSED
  1 equating varna, jati, caste, untouchability | 2 a timeless Gandhi |
  3 inferring blanket anti-varna from anti-untouchability | 4 Ambedkar
  reduced to untouchability | 5 Ambedkar reduced to reservation | 6 caste
  reduced to class or occupation | 7 Poona Pact narrated without autonomy or
  coercion | 8 "Harijan" treated as uncontested | 9 Article 17 or the 1989
  Act read as proof caste ended | 10 Gandhi-as-conscience plus
  Ambedkar-as-law complementarity | 11 flattening the periodisation |
  12 "Harijan" used as the narrator's neutral term | 13 Ambedkar reduced to
  constitutional drafting | 14 educate-agitate-organise as self-help |
  15 the Pact without the representation question | 16 romanticising village
  or central modernity | 17 collapsing caste into class or adding caste and
  gender arithmetically | 18 equating oppressed-caste assertion with
  hereditary supremacy | 19 treating redistribution, recognition and
  representation as substitutes | 20 letting convergence erase annihilation
  and autonomous rights.
        |
        v
THE QUALIFIED VERDICT -- ADAPT, NEVER REPRODUCE MECHANICALLY
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  | GANDHI'S METHOD REACHED THE CONSCIENCE OF THE DOMINANT, WHICH LAW   |
  | CANNOT COMPEL; AMBEDKAR'S REACHED THE STRUCTURE OF POWER, WHICH     |
  | CONSCIENCE CANNOT BE RELIED UPON TO SURRENDER. SOCIAL DEMOCRACY     |
  | REQUIRES BOTH, BUT ONLY THE SECOND CAN BE INSTITUTIONALLY SECURED.  |
  +--------------------------------------------------------------------+
  ASYMMETRIC FORM -> the moral condemnation of untouchability is common
  ground; the disagreement is whether caste ITSELF is the disease -- and on
  that question the reform-from-within position cannot explain why the
  hierarchy survives every reform of its outward practice.
  BEYOND-SAFEGUARD FORM -> reservation addresses access to positions;
  annihilation addresses the valuation of persons. Safeguards without
  constitutional morality, fraternity and economic independence remain
  formal -- which is Ambedkar's own diagnosis, not a criticism of him.
